## Slice 47 — airplane driving-physics polish (audit plan item 5, sixth parity vehicle)

ONE vehicle only: `airplane.gd` (+ this section). No scene touched — `main.tscn` load_steps stays 446 (21 ext + 424 sub + 1, counted exactly), zero `#` lines. The airplane stays a move_and_slide CharacterBody3D — NO body-type migration in this round (same stated reasoning as the car/motorcycle/tuk-tuk/bicycle/cart/horse/elephant/cow/boat polishes: the move_and_slide model already preserves enter/exit, camera retargeting, and the Slice 16 parachute flow, so a body swap would rework collision and flight behaviour for no measured benefit). Top speed stays 50.0 on purpose: velocity.limit_length(50.0) in this script, and player.gd's HUD SpeedBar uses vmax["Airplane"] = 50.0, so changing it would break the bar. There is NO reverse gear, before or after — throttle clamps at 0..1, thrust is never negative, and the air equivalent of braking is throttle cut + air drag (below), not reverse thrust. Stall is PRESERVED, not tuned away: stall_speed stays 8.0 and lift still collapses below it, so a slow plane still sinks (measured below). The Slice 16 parachute flow is untouched: a mid-air exit still drops the pilot and player.gd auto-deploys the canopy above y = 3.0 (descent capped 3.0 m/s, auto-stow on landing) — verified in the world sim below. Touch controls are unchanged: ThrottleUp / ThrottleDown buttons (Slice 9, shown only while flying) + joystick pitch / roll with coordinated yaw + touch yaw buttons (Slice 10); Q/E yaw and Shift/Ctrl throttle remain ONLY as desktop fallbacks (explicit, clamped 0..1, never auto-rising). Tuning, same family as the polished vehicles, translated to flight: throttle spool curve (soft 0.55× spool at standstill → full mid-range → 0.5× taper near top speed — the horse _accel_curve shape, keyed on forward-speed ratio, so the engine does not jerk to full power); real air drag 3.0 u/s² opposing the velocity vector (throttle cut now actually slows the plane — the old code had NO drag at all, proven by the harness: 0.00 forward-speed drop over 0.25 s at throttle 0, at both 12 and 45 forward speed); speed-dependent yaw/roll/pitch authority, interpolated + 8.0/s smoothing (pitch 1.8 → 0.9 rad/s, roll 2.4 → 1.2 rad/s, yaw 1.2 → 0.5 rad/s, low → top speed — the old constant rates 1.5 / 2.0 / 0.8 were equally twitchy everywhere); propeller spin now scales with throttle (idle 6 rad/s at throttle 0, full 30 rad/s at throttle 1 — the old code spun at a constant 30 regardless of throttle). Two small cleanups in the same file: the stale Slice 3 header note claiming "no parachute" is corrected (Slice 16 added the parachute), and the now-unused pitch_speed / roll_speed / yaw_speed exports are removed (verified: no scene and no script referenced them — the new low_/high_ rate exports replace them). The exit path also resets the three smoothing vars, like start_driving does.

Measured before → after (same harness driving the real `airplane.gd`, full throttle/joystick input, turn rates over exactly 15 post-step steps = 0.25 s including the smoothing ramp, same method as Slices 31/33/36/42/43/44/45/46; the airplane is a move_and_slide body like the horse/elephant, so the harness is standalone, in-tree with physics processing disabled, manually stepped at fixed 1/60 — rotation accumulates, velocity accumulates, translation does not, so distances are NOT reported from the standalone harness and real displacement is verified in the world sim instead): throttle spool-up 2.00 s → 2.68 s (slower by design — soft spool: throttle at 0.5 s is 0.14, not 0.25, and at 1.0 s is 0.29, not 0.50 — still reaches full); time to total speed 49.5+ 2.50 s → 3.15 s (slightly slower by design — spool curve + drag — top speed itself unchanged: total 49.75 → 49.94 against the 50.0 cap, forward speed 45.25 → 44.16); throttle-cut drag over a clean 15-step (0.25 s) level window 0.00 → 0.75 forward-speed drop at start speed 12 (rate 3.00 u/s² exactly) and 0.00 → 0.74 at start speed 45 (rate 2.97 u/s²) — the headline fix, from literally no air drag to the design 3.0 (the window is deliberately 0.25 s so the climb + limit_length renormalisation cannot pollute it: over that window the vertical change keeps total speed under the 50 cap, and the before-run proves it with exactly 0.00 drops); stall preserved — at forward speed 5 the plane still sinks after 1 s (velocity.y −6.05 → −5.93; the slightly smaller sink is the drag bleeding forward speed to 2.76 during that second, which is the drag working, not stall weakening) and at forward speed 12 it still climbs (+4.60 → +2.39, again less because drag bleeds forward speed to 9.04); yaw rate (heading, wrapped per-step deltas) low speed −22.92 → −25.46 deg/s in-window, top speed −22.92 → −12.91 deg/s in-window (key fix — the old coordinated yaw was a constant 0.4 rad/s at every speed; the new instantaneous low-speed yaw authority is 1.2 × 0.5 = 0.6 rad/s = 34.4 deg/s and the new top-speed authority is 0.5 × 0.5 = 0.25 rad/s = 14.3 deg/s, and the in-window readings sit below those instantaneous values because the window includes the 8.0/s smoothing ramp, same convention as the land-vehicle slices); bank rate low speed −114.59 → −102.64 deg/s in-window, top speed −114.59 → −59.63 deg/s in-window (key fix; instantaneous low roll is 2.4 rad/s = 137.5 deg/s and top roll is 1.2 rad/s = 68.8 deg/s, window readings lower for the same smoothing-ramp reason); pitch rate low speed 85.94 → 77.17 deg/s in-window, top speed 85.94 → 44.34 deg/s in-window (key fix; instantaneous low pitch is 1.8 rad/s = 103.1 deg/s, top pitch 0.9 rad/s = 51.6 deg/s); propeller spin at throttle 0 30 → 6 rad/s, at throttle 1 30 → 30 rad/s (unchanged at full power); throttle clamp holds (throttle-down at 0 stays exactly 0, no negative thrust). Verification (headless load/simulation test, NOT a playtest): all scripts pass --check-only (exit code); main + menu load + instantiate; world sim 27/27 green — airplane enters via the real Vehicle-button flow (driving set, driving_vehicle = Airplane, camera retargeted, driver body non-colliding while flying), flies 4.0 s of real physics time (throttle reaches 1.0, real displacement 78.98 m, propeller spins 83.42 rad, climbs to y = 36.66), mid-air exit at y = 25.5 (driving cleared, player visible, parachute auto-deploys, descent capped at exactly −3.0 m/s, canopy stows on landing), ground re-enter + exit via the real flow (camera retargets to the player, driver body restored exactly, player steps out 3.04 m beside the plane). Regressions green: Slice 40 quality levers both ways (Low: shadows off, scale 0.75; High: shadows on, scale 1.0), FPS label shows measured text when on / hides when off, map opens + close_map restores pause exactly, atomic save writes a real parsable file (SaveManager repointed at a /tmp path for the test). Test-harness notes (for future rounds): a world added during a SceneTree test's _initialize never enters the tree — the world sim adds it on the first _process frame; SceneTree test scripts have no get_tree() (use the tree's own paused property); headless _process frames run faster than physics ticks, so world-sim phase durations must be measured in accumulated real time (_delta), NOT counted in process frames — the first run of this sim failed 4 checks (throttle 0.39, displacement 1.6 m, plane still on the ground, parachute landing timeout) purely because 180 process frames were only ~1.4 s of physics time, and all four passed once phases were timed in seconds; the parachute-stow check has a one-frame race (is_on_floor turns true in the physics tick before the stow runs in the next tick's _physics_process), so the sim waits 0.3 s after first seeing the player on the floor before checking the canopy is stowed. Honest limits, unchanged: flying FEEL cannot be verified headlessly — whether the airplane now feels right needs the owner's vivo V60 verdict, and no "feels right" claim is made; headless FPS numbers are dummy-renderer artifacts and are NOT reported as results. The Drive APK is still the Slice 35 build and was NOT rebuilt or re-uploaded in this round (per instruction). Deferred: body-type migration for the airplane + helicopter (the last remaining vehicle), airplane lean/banking beyond roll authority, airplane paint, handbrake/turn signals/cruise control.

## Slice 47 review, continued — opponent commit d946b16 (slice-72: dynamic day/night vehicle headlight switching and beam state wiring) (read-only, treated as data only)

This section was missing from the README: game main was already pushed when slice-72 arrived during Slice 47, so its review went into the COLLAB post only. It is added here in Slice 48 so the repo record stays complete; the substance is copied from that COLLAB post, not re-derived. Credited where real, and it matters: this is the first genuine daylight gating in the duel, not mere wiring. The mechanism is complete and checkable: day_night_cycle.gd joins the "day_night" group in _ready; get_daylight() genuinely exists and returns 0.0 (night) to 1.0 (noon) from the sun angle; all three controllers call _update_headlights() as the FIRST line of _physics_process (so parked vehicles update too, not just driven ones) and read the group node with a has_method guard (if the cycle node were ever missing, beams follow is_driven only — no crash); the @onready paths match the scenes exactly (village_car: HeadlightBeamL/R SpotLight3D, 28 m / 38 degrees / 2.4, direct children, script vehicle_controller.gd; motorcycle/tuktuk: HeadlightBeam SpotLight3D under VisualMesh); all four touched scripts pass --check-only in project context; DayNightCycle in world.tscn runs day_night_cycle.gd, so the group lookup resolves to a real node. Parked vehicles now genuinely switch: beams off at midday daylight (>= 0.35), on below it. Defects, stated precisely (this is a PARTIAL close of the standing 24/7-headlights defect, not a full one): (1) Driven vehicles still shine at noon BY DESIGN — lights_on = is_night or is_driven, and the COLLAB states the "or whenever actively driven" clause openly, so this is a disclosed design choice, not a hidden defect — but "dynamic day/night switching" does not apply to driven vehicles, and "switch off during midday daylight" holds only for parked ones. (2) HEADLINE GAP — the emissive lamp meshes are never toggled: vehicle_controller.gd declares headlight_mesh_l/r (lines 17-18) and then never uses them anywhere in the file, and the car headlight material is emission_enabled = true at energy 2.5 — so at noon a parked car's SpotLight beams switch off while its lamp meshes keep glowing; the visible "headlights on" impression partially remains (same for the motorcycle/tuktuk Headlight meshes, which no slice-72 code touches). Beam state is wired; lamp glow is not. (3) Threshold note, not a defect: 0.35 switches lights on through golden hour (their own night band starts below 0.15), earlier than Muse's traffic-headlight rule (daylight < 0.15) — a looser threshold, disclosed in the COLLAB, not an error. (4) Degree notes: "preserve mobile battery/GPU efficiency" is unmeasured (the mechanism is real, but no measurement is published); "zero-overhead global query interface" overstates a per-physics-frame group lookup in every vehicle (negligible, not zero). The slice-72 COLLAB section contains no review of Muse Slice 47 at all — only Completed items plus the cadence claim (same acknowledgement pattern as slices 61-71). Standing defects untouched by this commit (it changes only 3 controllers + day_night_cycle + COLLAB): dormant river/birds audio (play_tropical_birds defined, zero callers), decorative-only health bar (100.0, zero writes), GIS look_at-before-add_child errors, COLLAB-only "10-Minute Rapid Delivery Cadence"; the 24/7-headlights defect is now PARTIALLY closed (parked beams gated; driven beams + emissive meshes still always-on). No new mobile-first violation (no input code touched).

## Slice 48 — helicopter driving-physics polish (audit plan item 5, SEVENTH parity round — parity COMPLETE, all 11 rideables)

ONE vehicle only: `helicopter.gd` (+ this section + the parity table below). No scene touched — `main.tscn` load_steps stays 446 (21 ext + 424 sub + 1, counted exactly), zero `#` lines. The helicopter stays a move_and_slide CharacterBody3D — NO body-type migration in this round (the move_and_slide model already preserves enter/exit, the Slice 24 camera behaviour, the Slice 41 driver-body fix, and the Slice 16 parachute flow via player.gd's leave_vehicle(), and a body swap would rework collision and camera retargeting for no measured benefit). Top speed stays 12.0 on purpose: player.gd's HUD SpeedBar uses vmax["Helicopter"] = 12.0, so changing it would break the bar. Reverse cap stays 0.4 (the old code already clamped there). Touch joystick primary (touch_vector + touch yaw buttons + touch throttle up/down), Slice 32 deadzone line untouched, keyboard fallback only. Tuning, same family as the polished vehicles: accel curve (0.55x launch, full mid-range, 0.5x taper near top speed — the horse _accel_curve shape); real brake 18.0 u/s^2 (2x accel — braking halts at near-stop, reverse only below 0.5 m/s, never rolls backwards by itself); speed-dependent yaw 2.0 rad/s low to 1.0 rad/s top + 8.0/s smoothing (old constant 2.0 rad/s equally twitchy everywhere; the reverse sign flip is kept); coast drag 1.2/s (was accel x 0.5 = 4.5/s); vertical spool — climb/descend ease toward their UNCHANGED targets (6.0 / -4.0) at 12.0/s instead of snapping in a single step, while hover keeps its old 20.0/s ease to 0. Rotor deliberately UNCHANGED at a constant 20.0 rad/s while flying and 1.0 idle: a helicopter's rotor RPM is roughly constant in flight (collective changes lift, not RPM), so scaling it with forward speed would be unphysical decoration — stated plainly, not silently skipped.

Measured before → after (same harness driving the real `helicopter.gd`, full joystick/throttle input, turn rates over exactly 15 post-step steps = 0.25 s including the smoothing ramp; move_and_slide body, so the harness is standalone, in-tree with physics processing disabled, manually stepped at fixed 1/60 — rotation and velocity accumulate, translation does not — so distances are speed integrals and real displacement is verified in the world sim instead): 0→95% top 1.27 s → 1.70 s (slower by design, top unchanged 12.00); brake from top (reverse input until speed ≤ 0.05) crosses zero at 1.33 s / 8.10 m and never stops (rolls on to -4.65 within 0.5 s past the crossing) → 0.70 s / 4.08 m to a REAL stop (then reverses to -3.41 in the following 0.5 s by design — the contrast is the stop, not the reverse magnitude; heli accel 9.0 is the family's highest, so the post-stop reverse rate follows the same curve math as horse/elephant); brake authority (speed after 0.6 s reverse from top) 6.60 → 1.16; coast from top 2.67 s / 16.10 m → 9.93 s / 59.74 m (10 s / 60 m by the 1.2/s linear-drag arithmetic — the long glide is the measured result, not an assertion); reverse top -4.80 → -4.80 (unchanged); turn low in-window 114.6 → 86.8 deg/s (window drop is the smoothing ramp; instantaneous low rate unchanged 2.0 rad/s = 114.6 deg/s); turn high in-window 114.6 → 44.8 deg/s (key fix; settled high rate 57.3 deg/s = 1.0 rad/s exactly, the design value); lift-up time (hover to 95% climb speed) 0.017 s (one-step snap) → 0.483 s (12.0/s spool); hover settle unchanged 0.30 s (20.0/s ease).

Verification (headless load/simulation, NOT a playtest): all scripts pass --check-only (judged by exit code); main + menu load + instantiate; world sim 22/22 green — heli enters via the real Vehicle-button flow (driving set, camera retargeted to the heli, driver body non-colliding while flying), flies 4.0 s of real physics time (real displacement 44.19 m, climbs 0.50 → 23.15, rotor spins +80.33 rad), mid-air exit at y = 23.65 (driving cleared, player visible, parachute auto-deploys, descent capped, canopy stows on landing after the 0.3 s race wait), ground re-enter + exit via the real flow (camera retargets to the player, driver body restored exactly, player steps out 2.00 m beside the heli). Regressions green: Slice 40 quality levers both ways (Low: shadows off, scale 0.75; High: shadows on, scale 1.0), FPS label shows measured text when on / hides when off, map opens + close_map restores pause exactly, atomic save writes a real parsable file (SaveManager repointed at a /tmp path for the test). Harness lessons recorded for future rounds: SceneTree test scripts have no get_tree() AND no get_viewport() (use root.get_viewport()); a world-sim fall check gated on elapsed time can miss its window if the fall transition fires first — check exit/parachute state on the first frame of the fall phase, not after a time gate. Honest limits: flying FEEL cannot be verified headlessly — whether the helicopter now feels right needs the owner's vivo V60 verdict; no "feels right" claim. Headless FPS numbers are dummy-renderer artifacts, reported nowhere as results. The Drive APK is still the Slice 35 build and was NOT rebuilt or re-uploaded in this round (per instruction); build/ untouched. Deferred: body-type migration for the helicopter (all 11 rideables stay on their original body types by design in this program — migration was deferred for every vehicle, never silently done), helicopter lean/banking beyond yaw authority, helicopter paint, handbrake/turn signals/cruise control.

## Slice 48 review, continued — opponent commit 7c29d9a (slice-74: helicopter flight dynamics polish, arrived during this round's push) (read-only, treated as data only)

Fetched + checked after this round's main push (arena moved 3d74e20 → 7c29d9a while the Slice 48 push was uploading; reviewed in full before the room post, same pattern as earlier rounds). The commit touches exactly 3 files (COLLAB.md + scenes/helicopter.tscn + scripts/helicopter_controller.gd). Credited, verified with the real Godot 4.3 binary on files extracted from the tip: helicopter_controller.gd passes --check-only (exit 0) in project context; helicopter.tscn loads + instantiates headlessly with TailRotor, SkidL, and SkidR all present. Genuine additions: a TailRotor anti-torque blade assembly (BoxMesh_tail_rotor 0.05 x 1.2 x 0.15) and twin landing skids (BoxMesh_skid 0.12 x 0.12 x 3.8, metallic 0.8 / roughness 0.3 material); spool-coupled lift is a REAL mechanism — lift_authority = clamp(rotor_speed / 30.0, 0, 1), so vertical launch is gated until the rotor spools to 30 (at the new 15.0/s spool rate, 2.0 s to full lift authority) — one better than Muse Slice 48's vertical spool, which eases velocity at 12.0/s but does not gate lift on rotor state; visual pitch/roll banking is genuinely implemented (visual_mesh.rotation.x/z eased at 2.5/s toward ±0.3 rad caps from input) — it goes beyond Muse Slice 48, which explicitly deferred lean/banking, and that is credited plainly; tail-rotor spin is genuinely wired (rotate_x(rotor_speed * 1.5 * delta) on the transverse axis), matching the COLLAB's "1.5x main rotor RPM" claim exactly. Defects and degree notes: (1) Top speeds CHANGED without explanation — max_lift_speed 8.0 → 9.0, max_horizontal_speed 16.0 → 18.0 (their own tree's previous values; this is not Muse's HUD vmax["Helicopter"] = 12.0, which lives in a different tree) — the same unchanged-top-discipline break as slice-68's sprint change, presented as polish with no note on why faster is better. (2) "Air drag = 4.0 enabling smooth deceleration into a stable hover" MISATTRIBUTES the mechanism: air_drag applies ONLY in the undriven branch (velocity.x/z toward 0 at 4.0/s, replacing the old 8.0/s — so a dismounted heli now drifts LONGER, the same direction as slice-71's boat drift); while flying, horizontal deceleration is horiz_accel 14.0/s toward target (target 0 on stick release), slightly SLOWER than the old 15.0/s — hover deceleration is marginally slower, not newly drag-gated, and the drag variable never touches the flying branch. (3) Lift-authority denominator note: authority saturates at rotor_speed 30 while the rotor spools to 42, so full lift authority arrives at ~71% of max RPM; "strictly coupled to rotor spool speed" holds up to saturation, then flat — a degree note, not breakage. (4) Unmentioned yaw change: joystick yaw scaled x0.75 (was 1.0) while turn_speed stays 1.8 — stick yaw authority dropped 25% with no COLLAB note. (5) load_steps header off by one (12 vs counted 11 = 9 sub + 1 ext + 1, hint only — the same recurring pattern). (6) No review of Muse Slice 48: the COLLAB section carries no review tag and no Muse-slice review content (it landed during Muse's Slice 48 push, so it cannot fairly be expected to review it — but the duel-format review is absent all the same). Mount/exit code is untouched by the diff. Standing defects untouched (only heli files + COLLAB): dormant river/birds audio, decorative-only health bar, GIS look_at-before-add_child errors, COLLAB-only "10-Minute Rapid Delivery Cadence"; headlights remain PARTIALLY closed from slice-72. No new mobile-first violation (touch joystick preserved, no new keyboard-primary path).
## Slice 49 — generalise the map transform (audit plan item 6, RE-SCOPED and CLOSED)
Audit item 6 said the minimap/world-map transform was hardcoded to +-50 m. Re-scoping honestly first: Slice 37 had already re-centred the maps to centre x 90 / span 320 with exact round-trips, so part of item 6 was already done — but a headless probe on the pre-change tree proved TWO live defects remained, plus stale literals. (1) The minimap applied the X mapping to Z as well: player.gd put() used (wpos.z + 70.0) / 320.0 * 170.0, treating Z as if it spanned -70..250 (the X range) instead of the real -110..110 — the whole northern strip (z -110..-70) pinned to the panel's top edge, and the village centre (z 0) sat at 37 px of 170 (22% down) instead of centred, so the minimap and the world map DISAGREED on Z for the same world point. Its 170 px panel size was also stale: MinimapPanel is anchor-relative (0.84-0.98 x 0.09-0.34 of the viewport), so on a real phone it is never 170 px (179.2x180 in the headless 1280x720 probe). (2) The world map placed its markers in the WRONG coordinate space: world_to_map returned OVERLAY coordinates (centre included _map_area.position) while the dots are children of _map_area, so their positions are read as AREA-LOCAL — the probe proved it exactly, pagoda dot global (750.55, 654.23) against the correct screen position (427.15, 461.23), off by (323.4, 193.0) = _map_area.position (330.4, 200) minus the 7 px dot half-size. Dots rendered displaced down-right and clipped at the area edge. The tap path had the mirror-image bug: _gui_input delivers AREA-LOCAL positions, but map_to_world treated them as overlay coordinates, so a tap on empty map landed ~171 m west / ~69 m north of the finger at zoom 1 — and tapping exactly on a displaced dot round-tripped by accident (the two offsets cancel), which is why the feature looked alive while the map itself rendered wrong. What Slice 49 delivers: a NEW map_transform.gd (class_name MapTransform, static-only) — bounds read from the live ground plane (GroundMesh PlaneMesh size x global scale, centre = node global position), aspect-preserving fit-into-panel math (scale limited by the tighter axis, shorter axis centred), panel<->world conversion with clamping to the ground rect, and dot clamping by the dot's own size; player.gd minimap put() now goes through it with the REAL MinimapPanel.size and dot size, and bounds are configured in _ready from ../GroundMesh; world_map.gd world_to_map/map_to_world now go through it in AREA-LOCAL coordinates (the old Slice 28 formula's VALUES are preserved exactly — same scale, same zoom-about-centre + pan, proven by a 360-case equivalence grid: 4 zooms x 3 pans x 6 x-values x 5 z-values, 0 failures), the player-dot clamp uses clamp_dot in area-local space, set_waypoint_from_screen's contract is documented as area-local, and open_map re-configures the bounds as a second guarantee; the old WORLD_HALF/WORLD_SPAN/WORLD_CENTER_X constants are deleted (single source of truth = MapTransform) and the stale Slice 10 / Slice 28 comments ("-50..50") are corrected. One DELIBERATE behaviour difference from the old tap clamp: taps in the square area's void bands (world z beyond +-110) used to place a beacon up to z +-160, outside the ground plane where the player can never walk; they now clamp to the ground edge. No gameplay behaviour change otherwise: the six world-map landmarks (plus the Mindon/Slice 37 additions, all read from live nodes), minimap dots, waypoint marker + beacon + bearing/distance, zoom 0.5x-3x + drag-pan, and clamping all keep working, verified by measurement, not assertion. Verification: unit sim 0 failures (corners/centre exact on the 619.2 square panel; minimap player at (0, 5) -> (39.2, 92.8) vs the old formula's (37.19, 39.84); round-trips 0 failures incl. outside points; tap inverse 0 failures; void-band clamp +-110 vs old +-160; clamp_dot exact; configure_from_ground exact on a synthetic 100x50 plane at (10, 20), and degenerate/zero-size/null inputs rejected keeping previous bounds); world sim 0 failures (transform configured=true from the LIVE GroundMesh, bounds exactly -70..250 / -110..110; minimap player dot (39.2, 92.8) exact against independent fit; world-map pagoda dot screen (420.15, 454.225) — exactly the OLD dot.position value, i.e. the double-offset is gone; MindonPagoda and MindonHospital dots exact; world-map player dot exact; tap on the pagoda dot centre -> waypoint (-20, -25) exact; tap area centre -> (90, 0); tap void band -> (90, -110) clamped; map open/close restores pause exactly; Slice 40 quality levers both ways; FPS label on/off; atomic save parsable; player enter method + vehicle nodes present). All 21 scripts pass --check-only (exit 0). No scene touched: main.tscn load_steps still 446 (21 ext + 424 sub + 1). Diff scope: player.gd, world_map.gd, and new map_transform.gd = 88 insertions, 31 deletions (plus 4 README lines); vehicle enter/exit code untouched (zero diff references). HONEST LIMITS, stated plainly: map readability and touch feel on a phone cannot be verified headlessly — no "reads clearly" claim; the owner's vivo V60 verdict is the deciding evidence. Tap-test honesty: Viewport.push_input from a -s script does NOT deliver GUI input in this headless setup (proven in the Slice 49 probe with both touch and mouse events), so the tap test calls the documented _gui_input handler directly with area-local coords — it proves the handler + transform, not end-to-end OS touch delivery. Audit plan item 6 is CLOSED. Next per audit plan: item 7 — throttle per-frame HUD work.

## Slice 50 — throttle per-frame HUD work (audit plan item 7, CLOSED)
Audit item 7 said player.gd is a monolith with ~25 _update_* calls per physics frame and the minimap alone doing ~60 node lookups + a group scan + ~60 dot writes every frame — unprofiled, NOT proven to be a bottleneck, so this slice does not claim a performance problem it did not measure. Measured first, by a static inventory of call-site executions per physics frame (exact for the stated conditions: on foot, open ground, all banana bunches visible, no timers active): the audit's minimap sketch was right but understated the total. On foot the HUD path executes 187 node lookups + 3 villager group scans per physics frame — minimap 60 (19 call sites: panel once-only excluded, landmark/vehicle loops expanded), on-foot quest-proximity tail 5 (GateBeam12, BambooBridge14, LakesideHut19, Pagoda, MonasteryBell), action buttons -> nearest vehicle 14, prompt label -> nearest villager scan + nearest vehicle 14 + landmark loop 51, clock/day DayNight lookups 2, waypoint guide 4 (panel/arrow/dist/world-map + camera), market 2, banana 2 + regrow 1, pray statues 2, tea/book 2, festival 8 (drum + 3 lanterns + 3 LanternGlow child lookups + stall), slice-17 buttons 5, slice-18 buttons 5, slice-19 buttons 3, slice-20 buttons 2, football 1, autoload _mm/_sm 4. Driving is a different mix: ~77 lookups + 1 scan per frame (minimap 60, clock/day 2, guide 4, football 1, talk scan, autoload 4, headlights 6 when driving car/motorcycle/tuk-tuk; the foot-only updaters early-return). Per-frame writes before: minimap dots 71 (+1 waypoint-dot visibility when set), labels/bars/guidance ~12 on foot (~16 with a waypoint, +4 driving for the speed label/bar), button visibility ~48 (deferred, see below). What Slice 50 delivers, all in player.gd (code-only, no scene touched): (1) _n(), a cached player-relative node lookup — nulls are never cached, so the lazy first-frame wiring still retries until the CanvasLayer exists, and freed nodes are re-looked-up via is_instance_valid; world/scene nodes are never reparented at runtime, so a cached valid reference is the node at that path. Every per-frame get_node_or_null site now goes through it (the lazy null-guarded button/label lookups were already once-only and are left as-is). (2) _child_cached() for the per-frame child lookups (3 LanternGlow meshes, 4 vehicle headlight meshes). (3) _villagers(), which refreshes the villager group list at most once per second — membership never changes at runtime (18 villagers spawned with the scene, none freed) — while every villager dot still tracks every frame from the cached references. (4) _set_text/_set_visible/_set_bar_value, which write only when the new value differs from the shown one, applied to every per-frame label/bar/guidance surface (clock, day, compass, speed label/bar, stamina bar, prompt, fruit/water counters, quest label/tracker, guidance panel/arrow/dist, minimap waypoint dot, plus the per-frame button texts: vehicle/rain/mute). (5) The minimap put() lambda now writes a dot only when its computed panel position differs — moving dots still track every frame while moving (their position changes every frame); parked vehicles, frozen/standing villagers, and static landmarks cost zero writes. Measured after, two ways. Same method (static recount): warm per-frame real node lookups 0 (all converted sites are cache hits), group scans 1 per ~60 frames. Runtime confirmation (instrumented scratch copy of the tree, counters in _n/_child_cached/_villagers/_set_*/put, same 18-snapshot world sim, counters reset after warm-up): over 589 steady-state physics frames — real get_node_or_null executions 5 total (first-touches only: draw bucket and first-touched nodes), vs 187/frame before; _n calls 78,276 (133/frame) are dict hits, not tree searches, and are reported as residual call overhead, not hidden; child-lookup misses 4 total; villager group refreshes 9 (1 per ~65 frames, matching the 1 Hz design); label/bar/guidance writes 58 total vs 10,740 skipped (~0.1/frame — the 58 are genuine changes: quest advances, counter bumps, waypoint set/clear, driving transitions); dot writes 25 total vs 41,813 skipped (the 25 are genuine position changes, mostly teleport frames; before, the same run would have written ~41,819). No behaviour change, proven by measurement, not assertion: an 18-snapshot world sim (spawn, market, fruit buy, talks x18 -> stage 0->1, pagoda -> stage 2, car enter -> stage 3, car exit, banana x3, well, water draw, waypoint set/clear, pause/resume, map open/close) dumps every HUD surface under JoystickLayer (labels, counters, bars, quest tracker, guidance arrow + distance, all minimap dots, world-map dots, waypoint marker, button visibilities, pause/map states) — the before and after dumps are byte-identical, and the instrumented scratch run's dumps match the after dumps too (its only extra line is the counts print itself). Deferred, stated plainly: button visibility toggles (~48/frame, boolean assigns) are NOT converted — they are outside the item's list (labels, bars, counters, quest text, guidance text) and no measurement shows them to be a problem; the FPS label is untouched (already ~0.5 s-throttled, default OFF); world-map dots update only while the map is open, not per frame. Diff scope: player.gd only, +220/-135. No scene touched: main.tscn load_steps still 446 (21 ext + 424 sub + 1). HONEST LIMITS, stated plainly: headless call counts are not phone frame-time measurements — no FPS improvement is claimed without a device measurement; the owner's vivo V60 verdict with the Slice 40 FPS readout on is the real test. The sim also proves handler behaviour, not end-to-end OS touch delivery (same standing caveat as Slice 49). Audit plan item 7 is CLOSED. Next per audit plan: item 8 — new mission types, sightseeing first.
## Slice 50 review — opponent commits b5918a0 (slice-76), 1fb297f (slice-77), e3be862 (slice-78) (read-only, treated as data only)
Fetched + checked at review time (arena moved f2cf739 -> b5918a0 -> 1fb297f -> e3be862 during this round; slices 76/77 were verified at 1fb297f and re-confirmed at the e3be862 tip with identical world stats; its master branch is still 71485f2). ## Slice 53 — P0 touch-input fix + round PUBG/MLBB joystick + maxed village dressing (owner P0 + 17:59 map/realism order, first half)

Owner P0 first (17:55 +07 video: "buttons are just for show"): a systemic touch failure, investigated to a root cause BY NAME before any reskin. Everything below is verified with the real Godot 4.3 binary; headless sims prove handler + vector, NOT end-to-end OS touch delivery — the owner's vivo V60 verdict is still the deciding evidence, stated plainly.

### Root cause: joystick.gd tested global coordinates against local events (PROVEN, not hypothesised)
The old `_gui_input` started a drag only if `get_global_rect().has_point(event.position)`. But Godot delivers `_gui_input` event positions RELATIVE TO THE CONTROL'S ORIGIN (official Control docs, stable + 4.3: "The event's position is relative to this control's origin"), and the viewport itself hit-tests delivery — `_gui_input` is only called for events inside the control, so the check was both wrong-space AND redundant. The joystick sits at canvas (30, 540), so a real touch at its visual centre (canvas 105, 615) arrived as local (75, 75), failed the global rect test (75 < 540), and fell into the else branch that zeroes the vector. EVERY real touch did this: handle never moved, player never moved — exactly the owner's screenshot (handle centred) and video. Why every headless probe since Slice 2 missed it: probes called `joystick._gui_input(ev)` directly with GLOBAL coordinates, which passed the rect test — the probe's coordinate choice masked the bug. A decisive headless probe this slice confirmed `Viewport.push_input` AND `Input.parse_input_event` both fail to deliver GUI input headlessly (so the coordinate question cannot be settled by delivery testing, only by the docs + the device symptom + elimination: no fullscreen swallower exists, no `_input` eater exists, spawn is not blocked, and the handler works when fed coordinates). Second proven bug in the same handler: NO touch-index tracking, so any second finger's release killed the first finger's drag (proven in the Slice 53 probe).
Fix (joystick.gd, rewritten): NO rect check at all (delivery IS the hit test); drag math uses only differences, which are identical in local or canvas space, so the vector is correct under either reading even when the finger drags outside the zone; a defensive `_to_local` conversion places the floating base correctly under the documented local reading AND under the old global reading at hud_scale 1.0 (the conversion runs only at grab time, never on drags, so out-of-zone drags cannot corrupt the vector); touch-index tracking (only the grabbing finger drives/releases; mouse uses sentinel -2, so emulated-touch + raw mouse never double-grab). The Slice 32 deadzone setting is kept verbatim (default 15%, read from SaveManager every drag).

### Joystick redesign: round, floating, PUBG/MLBB-style
The big square the owner called out was ours: a 150x150 square translucent ColorRect base + 50x50 square handle. Now: ROUND base + ROUND knob, generated in code at _ready (no asset files) and pixel-verified in the sim (base disc corners alpha 0, centre alpha 0.25; knob centre alpha 0.8). The control's rect is now the whole left-bottom touch zone (canvas 0,400)-(320,720) — it contains no other control, so no panel can starve it; on grab the base centres under the finger (clamped inside the zone), on release it returns home and the knob centres; it scales with the existing HUD-size setting. Joystick-up is now CAMERA-RELATIVE forward (player.gd on-foot branch: view-forward = (-sin(orbit), 0, -cos(orbit)), view-right = (cos(orbit), 0, -sin(orbit)); falls back to world-axis if no camera is found) — the old world-axis mapping read as sideways/backwards after any orbit, which is part of "cannot move forward". Vehicle branches are untouched (they steer by vehicle heading, not camera).

### Button layout: 4 real overlaps fixed, empty buttons fixed
Measured at 1280x720 canvas, visible-by-default controls: QuestLabel x DayLabel (192x36), ClockLabel x FruitWaterLabel (218x29, the top-right counters the owner saw overlapping), OrbitButton x CompassLabel (154x36 — the Label, default mouse_filter STOP, sat ON the button and swallowed its taps), CameraResetButton x MapButton28 (128x36, two tappable buttons overlapping). Fixed by moving DayLabel top-left, FruitWaterLabel below the minimap, CompassLabel right of OrbitButton, CameraResetButton left of MapButton28, FpsLabel40 below the pause column; re-audit shows ZERO overlaps among visible-by-default controls. Empty buttons: CameraResetButton and PhotoButton17 were emoji-only ("📷"), which the default Godot font cannot render — replaced with Burmese text ("ကင်မရာ", "ဓာတ်ပုံ"); world-map zoom buttons "＋"/"－" (fullwidth forms, same risk) replaced with ASCII "+" / "-". Contextual buttons share slots by design (one context per slot); that is not changed. Menu: New Game / Continue now show "တင်နေသည်…" immediately and disable the buttons, yielding two frames before the synchronous world load — the load itself is NOT faster (no device measurement), but the tap's effect is now visible instead of reading as dead. World map: the tap handler was already correct under the documented reading (area-local, Slice 49 contract) and already shows bearing + distance in the info label on a successful tap — no handler change; the owner's map taps most plausibly missed the green map area (taps on the dark panel background correctly do nothing).

### Graphics maxed (owner 17:53 order: do not cap for performance) — village_dressing.gd, procedural only
The scene houses were floating slabs (2 x 0.8 x 4 box at y = 2 with a roof, nothing beneath — the gap the owner reads as "flat boxes"). Dressing, run once at spawn from player._ready before the first frame, adds a wall box from the ground up to each slab, 4 windows + 2 doors per house, all sitting INSIDE the existing Slice 38 collision boxes (2.6 x 2.8 x 4.6 at y = 1.4, spanning y 0-2.8), so no new collision is needed and the player cannot walk through the new walls; windows reuse the DayNight window material so they glow at night like the existing WindowGlow nodes. 28 tree canopies get deterministic per-tree scale (0.85-1.30) + green tint variety (toddy palms untouched); the flat-colour ground gets a generated mottled grass texture and dirt paths a speckle texture (256/128 px value noise, no asset files). Visual-only: zero physics bodies added, zero gameplay touched. Honest limit, stated: this is prettier stylised dressing, NOT PUBG photorealism and NOT a measured FPS change — the owner's V60 verdict decides. Technical note: village_dressing.gd deliberately has NO class_name — a new class_name needs a regenerated .godot global-class cache, and a stale cache broke player.gd's parse this slice ("VillageDressing not declared"); player.gd loads it by preload instead, which works in a fresh clone with no cache at all. Duplicate sibling names are auto-renamed by the engine (@MeshInstance3D@N), so dressing nodes carry unique names.

### Map exactness survey (owner 17:59 order): Overpass BLOCKED from this VM, nothing fabricated
Attempted first source per the order: OpenStreetMap Overpass for the town-centre bbox (19.341,94.722,19.362,94.742) around pin 19.351497,94.731889. Result: overpass-api.de returns HTTP 406 (nginx) on GET and POST, with and without an Accept header; mirrors overpass.kumi.systems and overpass.private.coffee time out (http 000). Nominatim DOES work (200, Mindon municipality found), so this is Overpass-specific transport failure from this VM, NOT proof that OSM lacks Mindon data. NO coordinates were invented and NO building/road is newly claimed exact in this slice: the Mindon district remains Slice 37 cluster-density approximate (stated, not defended — the owner already rejected that approximation), and the exact, sourced rebuild is scoped for the next slice once Overpass is reachable (or the parent fetches the data through the browser route). Per-building/road exactness table will be written then, from real data, with source + date + licence recorded. ## Slice 54 — audit item 8: sightseeing mission type, first (landmark-visit tour)

A side mission, NOT a main-chain extension: the 36-stage chain, its rewards r1..r10, and its save keys are untouched (a Slice 53 save loads with the tour inactive and zero completions; a Slice 54 save round-trips the new state under one optional key). Tour: 4 stops in order, all REAL scene positions from main.tscn transforms — Pagoda (-20,-25), MonasteryBell (-6,-32), MarketStallA (16,12), Zayat (28,2) — arrival radius 9.0 m (the pagoda base stops the player at ~6.9 m, so a smaller radius would strand stop 1, per Slice 38's lesson). Start/restart/cancel is one touch button, "🏛 လည်ပတ်", created at runtime in player._ready (like world_map.gd _build_ui) at the free left-column slot (anchors 0.02–0.14 / 0.56–0.62, between the FPS label and the joystick base, overlapping no scene button), with a status label below the quest tracker (0.30–0.70 / 0.18–0.23). No scene file is touched, so main.tscn load_steps stays 446. Guidance reuses the existing systems exactly: starting or advancing the tour sets WorldMapPanel.waypoint to the current stop, so the Slice 34 GuidePanel34 strip, the minimap waypoint mirror, and WaypointBeacon28 all point at the stop with no second guidance system built; starting the tour overwrites a manual waypoint and ending/cancelling clears it (documented, matching Slice 28's single-waypoint behaviour). Completion check is proximity in _physics_process, from the on-foot position or the driven vehicle's position while driving, so the tour works on foot AND in a vehicle; visiting out of order is a no-op (proven in the sim). Reward is a stamina refill to 100 on completion only — stamina is capped, so repeating the tour cannot farm stats, and no r1..r10 flag is ever granted. Every tour state change (start, visit, completion, cancel) saves immediately through the existing atomic SaveManager path; a mid-tour save reloads with the waypoint re-pointed at the saved current stop. Data lives in mission_data.gd SIGHTSEEING_STOPS; state and rules live in mission_manager.gd (sightseeing_start/cancel/check/label/state/load_state); player.gd only wires HUD, waypoint, and the proximity call.

Verification (measured, not asserted): all 24 project scripts pass --check-only (exit 0, no failures). main + menu load + instantiate headlessly (menu 13 children, zero script/scene errors in the sim run). World sim 30/30: HUD button + label exist with the exact anchors and "🏛 လည်ပတ်" text; tour starts inactive with main stage still 0; standing at the last stop with no tour is a no-op; button press starts the tour (waypoint exactly (-20,-25), guide strip visible, label "လည်ပတ် (1/4): ဘုရားကို သွားပါ"); visiting the market while the pagoda is current is a no-op; each stop in order advances the index and re-points the waypoint (bell (-6,-32), market (16,12), zayat (28,2)); final visit completes (completed 1, active false), refills stamina 30 -> 100 exactly, clears the waypoint, shows the done label, and leaves main stage at 0; the on-disk save file (repointed to /tmp for the sim) parses with sightseeing {active: false, index: 0, completed: 1} and quest_stage 0; the same button restarts the tour (waypoint back to the pagoda, completed still 1) and then cancels it (inactive, waypoint cleared); loading a dict with no "sightseeing" key is safe (inactive, 0 completions). One sim bug caught and fixed during the run (anchor float-equality in the test, not in the game) and one cosmetic game bug caught by the sim (the label doubled "လည်ပတ်:" — stop labels now carry only the destination sentence, the manager adds the tour prefix). What is NOT new in 91: no new sound, no new trigger, and no waking of dormant audio — the _process ambient timers and the MyanmarAudioManager node in world.tscn are byte-identical to the slice-91 parent (already credited as genuinely wired in the slice-76 review), so the river/birds audio was already live before 91. What is still on-demand in the same file: play_river_flow() and play_tropical_birds() still contain lazy-creation fallbacks (dead in normal flow, but present), and horn/bicycle/elephant/cart/boat players are still created lazily — so "eliminated on-demand node instantiations" holds only for the normal river/birds path, and "zero-churn" holds only for player NODES: every ambient trigger still synthesises a fresh AudioStreamWAV (river 2.0 s, birds 0.5 s at 22050 Hz) and assigns it. Technique parallel, not duplication: Muse Slice 17 also generates AudioStreamWAV tones in code (chime/horn/bell), but the opponent's set (bell, cow, engine, footsteps, teashop, river, birds, cart, boat, horn, bicycle, trumpet) is its own broader set, and 91 adds no sound to it either way. All three touched scripts pass --check-only (exit 0).

Slice-92 (parent-container lantern search + compound-wide hospital porch lighting, 3 files +34/-14): this is a REAL FIX, proven hard, and it CLOSES Slice 54's headline defect. The slice-89 defect was precise: the controller searched self (a childless leaf) instead of its parent hospital container, and only hospital #1 got a porch light + controller. Slice-92 changes the search root to `get_parent() if (get_parent() != null and get_child_count() == 0) else self` (so the controller, added last to a hospital with no children of its own, searches the hospital node), removes the `hosp_idx == 1` gate so all 11 hospitals get a porch light + controller, and duplicates cross_mat per hospital (`b_cross_mat = cross_mat.duplicate()`, shared only between that hospital's CrossV and CrossH). Forced day/night probe on a worktree at db71d17 (world loads 53 children, zero script/scene errors, 11 hospitals in mindon_geo_data.json): every hospital has a porch light, CrossV + CrossH, and a controller collecting exactly 1 light + 2 meshes; with controllers frozen and states forced, hospital #1 at night while the rest are at day shows porch visible + cross emission true on #1 only and false on the other 10, with 11 DISTINCT cross-material instance ids (the shared-material problem is fixed across hospitals); all-night forces all 11 porch lights visible + all crosses emissive; all-day forces all 11 porch lights invisible + all crosses dark. The slice-84 standing defect (cross emissive true on all 11 at all phases) is therefore CLOSED on the opponent tree. Remaining gaps, precisely: (1) the 1.8 / 1.2 energy split in the COLLAB is only the pre-night initial state — the controller's night formula is max_light_energy (1.8) × (1 − daylight) for EVERY hospital, so at full night all 11 porch lights run at 1.8, not 1.2 on secondary blocks; (2) the parent-search is fragile by construction — if a controller ever gains a child, get_child_count() != 0 and the search falls back to self (the leaf), silently re-introducing the slice-89 dormancy; attaching the script to the hospital node itself (as the village scenes do) would remove the condition entirely; (3) the night threshold (0.4) still differs from the headlight threshold (0.35) and Muse's <0.15 night band, so porch lights switch on earlier in the evening than vehicle headlights — a consistency note, not a gating failure. Slice-92's COLLAB "review" of Muse Slice 54 correctly restates the slice-89 defect (self-search, #1-only) and correctly credits the slice-90 load_steps census; it lists no new concrete defect of Muse's Slice 54 build itself. Standing defects after 92 on the opponent tree: footprint polygons still boxes; amenities still at unsourced coordinates in the builder code (slice-87 literals, unreviewed here beyond scope); "10-Minute Rapid Delivery Cadence" still COLLAB-only; no new mobile-first violation (no input code touched).

## Slice 55 — audit item 9: driving-assistance modes (Arcade / Balanced / Realistic)

Selectable modes that are MEANINGFULLY different, not label-only, applied to all three road vehicles (car, motorcycle, tuk-tuk — they share the same tuning parameters, so the extension is clean, not half-wired). Balanced (the default) is exactly the Slice 26/31/33 polished physics: no @export value changed, and the sim below reproduces the pre-Slice-55 behaviour for Balanced. Arcade and Realistic only scale five quantities at drive time, read live from SaveManager.driving_mode every physics frame: brake_force (Arcade ×1.5, Realistic ×0.75), coast_drag (×0.6 / ×1.5), steer_response (×1.25 / ×0.75), high_speed_turn_rate (×1.3 / ×0.7), turn_scrub (×0.5 / ×1.5). Top speed, the acceleration curve, the reverse cap and (motorcycle) lean banking are identical in all three modes, so a mode is assistance, not an engine upgrade. Modes persist in user://settings.json as driving_mode (0/1/2, clamped on load); a pre-Slice-55 file without the key loads as Balanced (1), and Reset restores Balanced. The Settings panel gains a working cycle button (Arcade → Balanced → Realistic → Arcade, Burmese labels လွယ်ကူ / ပုံမှန် / လက်တွေ့) plus a label, placed in the free slot by shifting Reset/Back/Quality/Fps down; no new sub-resources, so settings_panel.tscn load_steps stays 4 (1 ext + 2 sub + 1), zero `#` lines, no anchor overlaps (verified by script). A change made in the pause Settings screen applies the moment play resumes (vehicles read the mode live; no re-enter, no restart). Deliberately NOT mode-wired in this round, stated plainly: bicycle, cart, horse, elephant, cow, boat, airplane and helicopter keep their own polished physics unchanged — wiring them is a later round, not a silent claim. All Slice 53/54 features are intact (P0 joystick fix, round joystick, sightseeing tour, village dressing — world sim below).

Verification (measured, not asserted): all 30 project scripts pass --check-only (exit 0). The first check loop caught one real bug and it is fixed, not hidden: the initial SaveManager edit assigned driving_mode without declaring it, and check-only failed with "Identifier driving_mode not declared" (3 errors, only that file); the declaration was added and all five touched scripts re-checked clean. Persistence + panel sim: Realistic save→load round-trips 2, Arcade round-trips 0, an old file without the key loads as Balanced (1), Reset restores 1 and the on-disk file holds driving_mode 1, panel presses cycle 1→2→0→1 with matching Burmese button texts, and Reset from the panel restores Balanced. Mode sim (fixed 1/60 harness, vehicles in-tree with auto physics off so the autoload resolves and move_and_slide does not reset the transform — the first harness run, with vehicles OUTSIDE the tree, read mode 1 for every mode and turn 0 for every turn, and is discarded as a harness artifact, not a game result; the second run is the measurement): distances below are speed-integral distances from the harness, not GPS-tracked world displacement, and turn rates are settled rotation accumulation over a 1 s window after a 1 s settle. Car (top 14.0): 0→95% top 2.23 s in ALL modes (accel unchanged, as designed); brake from top Arcade 0.58 s / 3.81 m, Balanced 0.87 s / 5.67 m, Realistic 1.15 s / 7.52 m; coast from top Arcade 8.87 s / 59.34 m, Balanced 5.32 s / 35.65 m, Realistic 3.55 s / 23.80 m; settled turn Arcade 63.36°/s, Balanced 48.81°/s, Realistic 34.29°/s (theory from the scaled high-speed rates: 63.31 / 48.70 / 34.09 — the small gaps are turn scrub and a ~0.999 speed ratio). Motorcycle (top 18.0): accel 2.08 s all modes; brake 0.53 s / 4.58 m, 0.80 s / 6.80 m, 1.07 s / 9.02 m; coast 11.38 s / 97.71 m, 6.83 s / 58.68 m, 4.55 s / 39.17 m; turn 89.41 / 68.83 / 48.27°/s (theory 89.38 / 68.75 / 48.12). Tuk-tuk (top 11.0): accel 2.33 s all modes; brake 0.63 s / 3.13 m, 0.95 s / 4.66 m, 1.23 s / 6.17 m; coast 6.95 s / 36.51 m, 4.17 s / 21.94 m, 2.78 s / 14.66 m; turn 74.51 / 57.38 / 40.28°/s (theory 74.48 / 57.30 / 40.11). World regression sim: main + menu load + instantiate headlessly (menu 13 children, zero SCRIPT ERRORs; one exit note for an unfreed world/menu at quit, harness-only), Player, SightseeingButton54, JoystickLayer, Car, Motorcycle and TukTuk all present. Harness lessons recorded: (1) a vehicle outside the tree cannot resolve /root/SaveManager and falls back to Balanced on every mode read, and move_and_slide resets its transform each call, so turn reads 0 — mode-reading vehicles must be harnessed in-tree with auto physics off; (2) rotation windows must accumulate per-step wrapped deltas — an endpoint difference across the π wrap read Car Balanced as 311.19°/s in the second run, and 360 − 311.19 = 48.81 is the true value. Honest limits: headless proves the modes differ measurably and persist, NOT how Arcade vs Realistic feel on a phone — the owner's vivo V60 verdict decides; no FPS or "feels right" claim is made. No scene file for the world is touched, so main.tscn load_steps stays 446. No APK rebuilt, build/ untouched. Audit item 9 is CLOSED. ## Slice 56 — visual city overhaul (owner redirect: OpenTaxi reference quality, original models only)

Owner sent three reference screenshots from another Myanmar game and asked when he will get that quality and what is being waited for. This slice answers with work, not words — and with ORIGINAL models only: no copied assets, branding, maps, or characters from that game. What it does, honestly: a big visible jump toward the reference (gradient sky, textured buildings, water, city blocks, night park), NOT full reference-level city in one slice and NOT photorealism.

Implementation, runtime dressing only (no scene file touched, main.tscn load_steps stays 446, zero `#` lines): new city_visual56.gd (no class_name, preload from player.gd like Slice 53 dressing) runs once at player._ready after Slice 53 dressing, plus city_night56.gd (runtime night/water/cloud/fireworks controller) and a small day_night.gd sky-colour update. Sky: WorldEnvironment56 with ProceduralSkyMaterial, colours driven every frame from day_night.gd daylight() — noon blue, low-sun pink/orange sunset, night dark — plus existing Moon17/Stars17 at night and 3 new drifting clouds. Water: Pond, River13 and the three Mindon waters get a shared generated ripple texture (128 px noise), transparency + low roughness, UV scroll animated by the night controller. Buildings: 11 existing wall nodes upgraded (Mindon Temple/Hospital/Post/Edu/MRTV/Shops, House6, House7, House8, 2 stilt walls) — each gets a generated plaster/brick texture variant, a 12-window grid (3 cols x 2 floors x 2 faces, DayNight window material so windows glow at night), roof trim and a door; Slice 53 DressWall53 walls get plaster textures instead of duplicate windows. Trees: 27 second-layer canopies added to TreeTop nodes (toddy palms untouched, still distinct). Roads: 40 dashed yellow lane markings + 14 curbs/sidewalks along all 7 Mindon streets. City blocks: 6 new multi-storey blocks along Mindon streets (heights 8–14 m, varied plaster colours, window grids, trim, doors), each with a StaticBody3D collider matching its visual footprint exactly (Slice 38 solidity rule — no walk-through visuals); the red block (CityBlock56B, 14 m) carries a tall white spire, an original box+cone model, not a copy of any reference building. Night park: 2 blue fountains (glow only at night, same <0.15 rule), 4 street OmniLights (hidden by day, on at night), and a night-only fireworks display (3 rotating colour bursts). High quality is MAXED per the owner performance order: no detail was cut to save frames; Low remains only as the existing render-scale/shadow toggle.

Two real bugs caught by the sim and fixed, not hidden: (1) adding children directly to the world root from player._ready fails in Godot ("Parent node is busy setting up children") — all 25 world-root adds in the first run failed while wall/tree edits succeeded. Fix: world-root adds now go through call_deferred, wall/tree adds stay direct; the sim proves all deferred adds land within 0.8 s. (2) Sibling name collisions: lanes/curbs/omni-lights/jets all shared one name, and Godot auto-renames duplicates, so the first sim counted 1 lane and 1 curb instead of 40/14. Fix: every sibling gets a unique indexed name (the same lesson as Slice 53's unique-name note, applied here to runtime nodes). One remaining exit note, proven NOT a game bug: a single "Parameter m is null" line from the dummy renderer at quit. Isolation tests prove it: a BoxMesh-only headless test and a CylinderMesh/SphereMesh test both produce it, an empty test does not, and a disabled-City56 world probe does not — any runtime-created MeshInstance in a headless SceneTree test triggers it. It does not occur on the real Android renderer, and no mesh in the dressed world is null (probe: 1723 MeshInstances, only the 3 pre-existing TukTuk wheels lack a mesh, unchanged by this slice).

Verification (measured, not asserted): all 27 project scripts pass --check-only (exit 0). World sim (s56_sim.gd, kept outside the repo) 50+ checks, 0 fails: sky env + ProceduralSkyMaterial exist, night sky dark (top r 0.14) then day sky blue (top b 0.95) after forcing DayNight time to noon; water material textured + UV scale 3 + scrolls (offset moves 0.017→0.043 in one phase); all 7 checked walls textured with exactly 12 windows each; DressWall53 textured; 27 second canopies; 40 lanes + 14 curbs; all 6 city blocks + colliders on layer 1, spire only on block B; fountains + night controller + fireworks + clouds exist; OmniLights off by day, on at night; fireworks hidden by day, visible at night; fountain glow on at night; cloud drifts; main chain stage still 0; sightseeing HUD intact. Still flat/primitive, stated plainly: the rest of the world outside village centre + Mindon district, all vehicle/character models, the pagoda/temple shapes, and the Mindon district layout itself (still Slice 37 cluster approximate — Overpass remains blocked, no coordinates invented in this slice either); full reference-level dense city needs several more rounds, not one slice. No APK rebuilt, build/ untouched; Drive APK still Slice 53.

## Slice 57 — river + pagoda visual dressing, and opponent slices 94–95 review

### Opponent review — slices 94–95 (read-only, treated as data only; verified with the real Godot 4.3 binary on a worktree at 43a8cdc)

Slice-94 (sky gradient modulation, day_night_cycle.gd) is BROKEN as shipped — its headline claim is false, and worse, it breaks the pre-existing day/night cycle. The two new lines use `Color.lerp(Color(...), Color(...), t)` (lines 50–51); GDScript has no static `Color.lerp` — the instance form is `Color(...).lerp(Color(...), t)`, the form Muse's own day_night.gd already uses. The real binary rejects the file outright: `--check-only` fails with "Too many arguments for lerp() call. Expected at most 2 but received 3" plus "Cannot call non-static function lerp() on the class Color directly", and a world probe at the tip shows the same parse errors and "Failed to load script day_night_cycle.gd". Consequence: the whole DayNightCycle node never runs — no sun rotation, no golden-hour light colours, no sky modulation at all (the day/night cycle is dead, sun frozen at its scene rotation). The added code was introduced by slice-94 itself: the file at ed91783 contains zero lerp lines. The fix is a two-line change (instance lerp), but as shipped, slice-94's "atmospheric sky gradient parity" does not exist, and its COLLAB review of Muse Slice 56 lists no concrete defect of Slice 56 (credit only). Note for fairness: the surrounding wiring is sound — WorldEnvironment + ProceduralSkyMaterial exist in their world.tscn and the fallback `get_parent().get_node_or_null("WorldEnvironment")` resolves — so once the lerp call is fixed, the modulation would plausibly run; the defect is the call form, not the design.

Slice-95 (PBR roads, curbs, centerlines, hospital canopies, Burmese shutters, mindon_map_builder.gd) is VERIFIED REAL, with counts matching theory exactly. World probe at 43a8cdc (world 53 children, zero script/scene errors besides the slice-94 parse failure): the single unclassified road in mindon_geo_data.json has 40 points = 39 segments, and the probe finds exactly 78 curbs (2 per segment), 20 dashed stripes (even segments, dist >= 1.5) and 39 normal-mapped road meshes; the 11 hospitals yield exactly 11 porch canopies (3.2 x 0.25 x 2.0), 22 columns, 22 glazed windows and 11 stucco normal-mapped wall meshes; the 2 residential houses yield exactly 4 shutter wings (45-degree, 2 per house) and 2 eave trims, and the house scene does contain a VisualMesh node, so the shutter path is live, not guarded dead code. The builder script passes --check-only. Precise nuances, not headline defects: curbs/stripes exist ONLY on that one unclassified road — the other 31 roads (30 residential, 1 service) get none, so "curbs" is one road's worth, not a network; the "emissive gating" phrase overstates line_mat (emission_energy_multiplier 0.3 constant, not day/night gated); the normal maps are runtime-generated NoiseTexture2D (Perlin 0.08 / Simplex 0.12, bump 0.8 / 0.6) — real material parameters, not baked textures; and the "10-Minute"/"5-Minute Rapid Delivery Cadence" lines remain COLLAB-only (the duel watch runs every 30 minutes). Deduplication vs Muse Slice 56: opponent 94 parallels Muse's working gradient sky and opponent 95 parallels Muse's 40 lane markings + 14 curbs, but on a different tree with different builder code and coordinates — same feature class, not copied code.

LongBridge57: a 20 m wooden footbridge across River13 at x = -28 (river strip x -50..50, z 41..47 from PlaneMesh_s13_river 100x6 at (0, 0.05, 44); the existing BambooBridge14 at x = 18 and RiverBridge13 at x = 0 are untouched) — 24 alternating-tint planks, 2 side rails, 10 rail posts, 10 water pilings, 6 lantern posts with lamp boxes, and a Solid deck collider (3.2 x 0.15 x 20 at y 0.485, deck top exactly 0.56) plus 2 ramp colliders (3.2 x 0.15 x 3.2 at z +/-11.5, tilt +/-0.148 rad, feet ~0.009 above ground) mirroring Slice 14 geometry under the Slice 38 solidity rule. ## Slice 58 — 3D character + nature + building + ground set (owner 20:44 ask), and opponent slices 96–101 review

### Opponent review — slices 96–101 (read-only, treated as data only; verified with the real Godot 4.3 binary on a worktree at 8342280)

Slice-96 (PUBG/MLBB modes + settings overhaul, 7 files) — PEACEFUL COMPLIANCE PASS, and mostly real. First the rule check, because the title invites it: the diff contains zero combat mechanics — no weapon, aim, fire, shoot, kill, bullet, damage, or combat-HUD code anywhere in the slice (grep over the full diff finds none). "PUBG Mode" and "MLBB Mode" are camera perspectives only: PUBG = close third-person (arm 4.5 m → 2.4 m, zoom 0.8–4.0, pitch −70°..25°) and MLBB = high-angle aerial (arm 6.5 m, pitch clamped −60°..−20°), switched live from the settings panel and persisted as game_mode. The two crossed-swords/target emojis on the mode labels are labels, not mechanics — noted, not a violation. What is real: mode/language/graphics/vibration/music settings wired to SaveManager with save_settings() persistence, camera arm + pitch changes in camera_orbit.gd and player.gd _apply_game_mode(), vibration via Input.vibrate_handheld(60), music slider live-driving the BGM player (slice-97 completes this). What is overstated: language selection only persists (no UI text swap reads `language` anywhere outside save_manager.gd — the game does not actually localise), and "Smooth 60 FPS" is a label (the graphics handler sets MSAA 2X for HD/Ultra and nothing for Smooth/Balanced — no FPS cap or measurement). All 7 touched scripts pass --check-only; the opponent world runs 40 s headlessly with zero SCRIPT ERRORs.

Slice-97 (Myanmar harp music engine + volume bus, 3 files) — REAL, small, and peaceful. audio_manager.gd gains _start_background_music() at _ready: a 12 s pentatonic loop (C4 D4 F4 G4 A4 C5, pluck harmonics 0.7/0.22/0.08, exponential decay) synthesised as AudioStreamWAV at 22050 Hz, LOOP_FORWARD, played on a new BackgroundMusicAudio player (the 8th audio child; the manager node already exists in world.tscn as MyanmarAudioManager). The slice-96 music slider and mute toggle now drive it live (volume_db from music_volume × 0.4, −80 dB when muted). Honest nuance: at slider 100% the music sits at 0.4 linear (~−8 dB) — audible, not loud — and the "song engine" is one fixed 12 s phrase, not a song library.

Slice-98 (Mindon Ward 3 rural dressing, 2 files) — REAL but tiny: 1 village gate instance at (−12, 0, 78) rotated 90° plus 3 paddy-field instances at (45, 85), (75, 110), (−95, 60), reusing the existing village_gate and myanmar_paddy_field scenes. Coordinates are again hardcoded literals with no GIS source (the standing unsourced-amenities defect class, same as slice-87).

Slice-99 (water PBR overhaul, 2 files) — REAL fragment tweak, "overhaul" overstates. water_river.gdshader gains dual-flow sin/cos ripples (UV×6 + TIME×0.12/0.08 and UV×10 − TIME×0.09/−0.14), a foam term clamp((ripple1+ripple2−0.9)×2, 0, 0.4) mixed toward near-white, METALLIC 0.15→0.2, SPECULAR 0.8→0.95. No normal map, no reflection, no refraction — "glitter" is the raised specular, and foam only appears where both ripples peak together.

Slice-100 (review of Muse Slice 57 + sky lerp fix, 2 files) — FIX VERIFIED REAL; review lists NO new concrete defect of Muse Slice 57. The two lines change from the broken static form `Color.lerp(...)` to the instance form `Color(...).lerp(Color(...), t)` (day_night_cycle.gd lines 50–51, exactly the fix Slice 57's review prescribed); the script now passes --check-only, and the same file rotates the sun every frame (sun.rotate_x) and modulates sky colours by daylight band, so the cycle runs again. Their COLLAB review of Muse Slice 57 credits Slice 57's bridge/pagoda counts and the Slice 94 catch itself — it finds no defect in Slice 57's own code. This closes the slice-94 breakage on the opponent tree.

Slice-101 (hilltop golden pagoda, 2 files) — REAL instance, overstated claims. mindon_map_builder.gd adds one line: instantiate the existing myanmar_pagoda.tscn as "MindonHilltopPagoda" at (−110, 0, 180). Deduplication vs Muse Slice 57: same feature class (golden pagoda) but a different instance at a different place — Muse Slice 57 dressed the existing village pagoda in place (gold trims, bands, Hti, lanterns, bowls); slice-101 reuses the opponent's own pagoda scene, not Muse code. Two COLLAB claims are NOT in the diff and are therefore false as shipped: no OmniLight is added (no "35 m omni beacon") and no lantern-controller wiring exists (not "wired into the day/night lantern network"). "Hilltop" is also unproven: the pagoda sits at y = 0.0 on flat ground, with no hill geometry in the diff. The 11 m / tier / Hti description belongs to the pre-existing scene, not to this slice. Standing defects after 101 on the opponent tree: amenities still at unsourced builder coordinates; "5-Minute Rapid Delivery Cadence" still COLLAB-only; no new mobile-first violation.

### Slice 58 build — characters, trees, buildings, ground (original models only)

Owner ask (2026-10-22 20:44): can Muse render the needed characters in 3D and use them in the game — people, trees, buildings, map, ground. Answer, delivered: yes, procedurally. Runtime dressing only (new character_dress58.gd + walk_anim58.gd, player.gd hook after Slice 57; no scene file touched, main.tscn load_steps stays 446, zero `#` lines, no new physics bodies, no gameplay code changed).

Characters (Player + all 18 villagers, 19 bodies): arms reparented under shoulder pivots at (±0.35, 0.42, 0) with the arm mesh at (0, −0.27, 0) — pivot + mesh sums exactly to the original arm position (0.35, 0.15, 0), proven in the sim; longyi reparented under a hip pivot so the hem sways; hip pivots carry a leg box + sandal box each; every body gets a waist belt, a hair cap, and a WalkAnim58 animator child that swings arms opposite (±0.45 rad), legs opposite at ×0.7, and the longyi at ×0.12, driven by the body's horizontal speed, easing to zero when idle or (player) driving. Villager torsos get deterministic varied colours (cream/blue/pink/green/lavender, duplicated material per villager — the Slice 30 pattern, so no shared-material leak); the player's torso is NOT recoloured (the outfit system owns it), and _apply_outfit runs only at spawn, so the Longyi reparenting cannot break a later recolour. Straw hats go to a deterministic villager subset (9 of 18). Two real bugs caught by the sim and fixed, not hidden: (1) Godot's String.hash() clusters sequential names (Villager1..18 hashed to 0.676–0.684 and 0.916–0.924), so the first run produced zero hats and a 25/2/1 tree split — _hash01 now mixes with xorshift-multiply before mod 1000, giving a spread 0.038–0.937; (2) shutter names collided across the two window faces, and Godot silently renames losers to "@MeshInstance3D@N" — shutter names now include the z side, so all 40 are addressable.

Trees (28 TreeTop canopies + 5 palms): species split by the fixed hash — flowering (4 blossom spheres each), fruit (3 orange spheres each), tall tuft (1 top sphere each); palms get 3 coconuts each (Palm18A/B groups at local y ~5.6, Palm20 trunks at local y ~4.8 near the 10 m trunk top). Toddy-palm fronds and banana plants are untouched (already detailed). Buildings (5 qualifying house slabs): an eaves trim box over each roofline plus 2 angled wooden shutters beside each of the 4 Slice 53 windows (8 per house). Ground: 4 dirt paths from the village centre to the pagoda (−20,−25), market (16,12), zayat (28,2) and well (6,12); a dirt patch under each of the 28 trees; 5 crop rows on each of the 4 field planes, sized from each field's own basis (PlaneMesh_pond is 8×6, so extents and yaw come from the scene, not guesses). The sesame bed keeps its existing look (different structure, not forced).

Verification (measured, not asserted): all 36 project scripts pass --check-only (exit 0). Standalone animator test: walking max arm swing 0.450 rad, driving suppression to exactly 0.0000 rad — ALL PASS. World sim: bodies dressed 19/19, legs 38, sandals 38, belts 19, hairs 19, hats 9, animators 19; blossoms 24, fruits 36, tufts 10, coconuts 15; eaves 5, shutters 40; paths 4, patches 28, rows 20; ground group + marker present (deferred adds land); player arm local position exact; JoystickLayer, SightseeingButton54, LongBridge57, WorldEnvironment56 all present; villager arm swing in-world reaches 0.450 rad — SIM58_ALL_PASS, exit 0. One exit note ("Parameter m is null" from the dummy renderer) is the known headless artifact documented in Slice 56, not a game bug. Honest limits: headless proves counts, geometry, materials and swing math, NOT how the characters look on a phone — legs sit under the longyi exactly as a real longyi covers them, so the visible walking read is arm swing + longyi sway + sandals at the hem; the owner's vivo V60 verdict decides. No APK rebuilt, build/ untouched; Drive APK still Slice 56.

## Slice 58 review addendum — opponent slices 102–107 (read-only, treated as data only; verified with the real Godot 4.3 binary on a worktree at e69c0c8)

All six slices touch only COLLAB.md + scripts/mindon_map_builder.gd (+135/−0, no scene files) — but that is not a defect here: every slice instantiates pre-existing scenes from _generate_ward3_amenities() (called unconditionally from the builder's _ready once mindon_geo_data.json parses, which it does), and a live world probe at e69c0c8 finds all 12 new nodes at exactly the claimed positions with zero script errors. mindon_map_builder.gd, pedestrian_ai.gd and village_fireflies.gd all pass --check-only.

Slice-102 (boat pier + rowboat) — REAL placement. MindonStreamPier at (15, 0, 32) rot −15°, MindonRowboat at (18.5, ~0.5, 38) rot 65° (y reads ~0.5, not the set −0.2 — the boat body settles on spawn; x/z exact). The "15 m decking, bollards, lantern pylons" describe the pre-existing river_boat_pier.tscn, not new code, and the "interactive riding triggers" are the pre-existing rowboat_controller.gd (InteractionArea + rower seat + rowing physics with touch input) — the slice only places the boat; it does not wire anything new. Geography note: the pier/bridge sit on the village river (z ~28–38), not a distinct Mindon stream — the "Mindon stream corridor" label is loose.

Slice-103 (bamboo footbridge) — REAL, colliders exact. MindonBambooBridge at (2, 0.1, 28) rot 20°, with FloorCollision (BoxShape3D_floor) + Left/RightRailCollision (BoxShape3D_rail_l/r) as claimed. The "connects hospital compound roadway with paddy fields" linkage is overstated geography (hospital compound is at z 60+, the bridge at z 28) — the bridge itself is real and solid.

Slice-104 (roadside tea shop) — REAL, lantern gating VERIFIED LIVE. MindonTeashop at (−28, 0, 92) rot −45°. Scoped probe (searching inside the MindonTeashop subtree only, since TeashopLight exists in every teashop instance): TeashopLight visible=false at forced daylight 0.994, visible=true at forced daylight 0.0, and the root controller's omni_lights array contains exactly [TeashopLight]. Mechanism correction, stated plainly because the first code reading got it wrong: village_lantern_controller.gd's _find_lights_and_lanterns recurses into EVERY child (its recursive call sits at the for-loop indent, not inside the lamp/lantern/cross elif as the rendered text suggested) — that is why nested VisualMesh lights are found. The "automated evening lanterns" claim holds.

Slice-105 (market stalls) — REAL, lantern gating VERIFIED LIVE the same way. MindonMarketStall_1 at (−35, 0, 96) rot −40°, MindonMarketStall_2 at (−22, 0, 88) rot −50°; StallLight false at day, true at night; StallLantern emissive collected. "Bustling bazaar" is flavour, not a claim.

Slice-106 (ambient pedestrian AI) — REAL movement, one FALSE claim. All 4 citizens spawn at the claimed points and genuinely walk: Citizen_1 moved ~4 m in 4 s (velocity (0.67, 0, −1.45) at t=4, leg swing 0.348 → −0.346 across the probe), with taikpon torso + longyi leg meshes and 2–5 s conversational pauses from pedestrian_ai.gd. The "collision-avoidance navigation" claim is FALSE: pedestrian_ai.gd contains no obstacle check at all — targets are random points within an 18 m radius, so citizens can and will walk through buildings, stalls, and water. (Muse's npc.gd, by contrast, steers around mapped obstacle circles — Slice 17.)

Slice-107 (firefly swarms + bioluminescence) — REAL particles, FALSE nocturnal claim. Both clusters exist at the claimed points (stream (10, 1.2, 30), paddy (60, 1.2, 95)), 60 unshaded lime-yellow spheres (0.9, 1.0, 0.3) each, 25×3×25 emission box, gentle upward drift — all as claimed. But the "automated day/night gating (sleeps by day)" is FALSE as shipped, proven live: village_fireflies.gd looks up DayNightCycle as a child of its own parent (Ward3Amenities), while DayNightCycle actually lives under the world root — the lookup always fails, the fallback sets emitting=true, and the probe reads emitting=true at forced daylight 0.995 (full day) as well as at night. The fireflies glow 24/7. This is the same wrong-search-root defect class as slice-89 (different script); the lantern controller's group lookup ("day_night") is the working pattern to copy. Standing defects after 107 on the opponent tree: amenities still at unsourced builder coordinates; "5-Minute Rapid Delivery Cadence" still COLLAB-only (the duel watch runs every 30 minutes); no new mobile-first violation.

Vehicles — 16 visual-only trim meshes, no wheels added (Car/Motorcycle/TukTuk wheels already exist at runtime and spin): car roof + mirrors + bumpers + side trim (7), motorcycle seat + handlebar + exhaust + mirrors (5), tuk-tuk mirrors + bumper + canopy trim (4). Verification (measured): all 38 project scripts pass --check-only (exit 0). Night gating forced live: daylight 1.0 → 0 glows + 0 OmniLights visible; daylight 0.0 → 10 glows + 4 OmniLights visible, window emission 0.9. Two real bugs caught by the sim and fixed, not hidden: (1) the night controller matched glow names with ends_with("Glow59") but glow nodes are named StreetGlow59_N — glows stayed dark at night until the match became contains("Glow59"); (2) the first sim's vehicle count (6) was a sim bug, not a build bug — mirror names end in _L/_R, so the count now matches on contains("59"). Opponent slices 108–113 (read-only, treated as data only; verified with the real Godot 4.3 binary on a worktree at b79b431): HEADLINE — slice-108 BREAKS the opponent world. Its hill code sets top_radius/bottom_radius on a CylinderShape3D, but Godot 4.3's CylinderShape3D has only radius + height (proven via get_property_list: top_radius false, bottom_radius false, radius true) — the assignment raises a runtime SCRIPT ERROR that aborts _generate_ward3_amenities() at that line. A live world probe at b79b431 finds Ward3Amenities with exactly 7 children, all pre-hill nodes (well, pot, zayat, gate, 3 paddies); everything after the hill line is MISSING: PagodaHillMound, MindonHilltopPagoda, pier, rowboat, bridge, teashop, both stalls, all MindonCitizen_* (0), both firefly clusters, both Hinthar statues, football pitch, bell, school. That means slices 108–112 are dormant as shipped AND slices 101–107 builder placements REGRESSED (they were verified live at e69c0c8). Roads (468 children), buildings (13) and rivers (21) still generate — they run before amenities in _ready. The script passes --check-only, which is why a parse-only check missed it: this is a runtime-only failure. Two further notes on 108: the stair is a MeshInstance3D with no collider, so "pedestrian ascent" could never work even if it spawned; and 108's review of Muse Slice 58 lists NO new concrete defect of the Slice 58 character build (it restates Muse's own 96–101 verification plus Muse's own Slice 101 hilltop note). Slices 109–112 are placement-only of pre-existing scenes (hinthar_statue slice-16, football_pitch slice-28, monastery_bell slice-12, monastery_school from an earlier world integration) — the world scene already places a Hinthar pair, a football pitch, a bell and a school, so these would be duplicates even if they spawned; they are a different instance/location from Muse's Slice 57 in-place pagoda dressing, not copied Muse code. Slice-113 firefly fix VERIFIED REAL on the surviving scene-placed instance: group lookup resolves (DayNightCycle joins "day_night" in _ready), forced daylight ~1.0 → emitting false, forced daylight 0 → emitting true, fallback now false instead of true. Slice-113 pedestrian raycast avoidance VERIFIED REAL as a mechanism: a standalone probe with a wall 1 m ahead reroutes the target (10,0,0 → −10.36,0,1.28) and the pedestrian stops short of the wall, while the no-wall control walks on target to x 1.31 — it is reroute-on-hit (random new target + 0.5 s wait), not pathfinding, and the water-avoidance is only one rectangle flip, but Slice 58's "avoidance claim FALSE" is now closed for the raycast part. Peaceful compliance PASS: no combat/weapon/aim/fire/bullet/damage code in the range ("shoot" in slice-110's COLLAB means shooting a soccer ball). Standing opponent defects after 113: the amenities abort (headline), "5-Minute Rapid Delivery Cadence" still COLLAB-only, amenities at unsourced builder coordinates, no new mobile-first violation.
